11 categories · 98 reference ideas · 81 detailed designs

Threat actor names, evidence labels and technique connections

Selected MITRE ICS profiles and related Dragos tracking names. Labels apply to the cited evidence scope. Technique connections support investigation and detection planning. A matching catalog record alone does not attribute an incident.

Demonstrated process disruption A cited incident documents disruption in an industrial process or safety-system environment.

Analyzed tooling capability Analysis documents what a tool can do. Applicability depends on the target and environment.

Access or espionage activity The cited evidence documents entry, persistence, reconnaissance, collection or interface access.

Sandworm Team · ELECTRUM Demonstrated process disruption

MITRE lists ELECTRUM with Sandworm. Dragos associates ELECTRUM with Sandworm and tracks its operational scope separately.

The 2016 Ukraine grid incident caused a power outage. Dragos attributes the control-system operation to ELECTRUM using CRASHOVERRIDE.

INCONTROLLER / PIPEDREAM · CHERNOVITE Analyzed tooling capability

CHERNOVITE is a Dragos activity group. The linked MITRE object is INCONTROLLER software, also called PIPEDREAM. The ICS snapshot has no separate CHERNOVITE intrusion-set object.

Dragos links CHERNOVITE to development of PIPEDREAM. The 2022 toolkit analysis documents capabilities against industrial devices, CODESYS, Modbus and OPC UA. The capability label refers to that analysis.

Profile evidence reviewed 2026-11-05. The technique paths are versioned snapshot relationships. Software paths describe associated tool capability and are labeled separately from group-to-technique paths.

How to read this catalog

These 11 categories organize detections by the behavior that triggers the rule or the OT function it protects. They are a custom taxonomy informed by ATT&CK Enterprise, ATT&CK for ICS, and OT detection engineering. They are not official MITRE tactics or mandatory attack stages.

Category describes what the rule detects; log source identifies the evidence it needs; ATT&CK mapping identifies related adversary behavior where supported. Select a category below to show only its entries, or select All categories to restore the full view. In each record, select an OTD ID under Traceability to open the corresponding source use case.

The 11 OT detection categories
CategoryPurposeExample OT detection
01 Exposure & Initial AccessIdentify exposed systems and attempts to enter OT.Unauthorized access to an internet-exposed engineering gateway.
02 Identity, Credentials & PrivilegeDetect credential abuse, authentication anomalies, and privilege misuse.An OT service account performs an interactive logon.
03 Execution & Runtime ActivityIdentify unexpected processes, scripts, and services.PowerShell launches unexpectedly on an engineering workstation.
04 Discovery & EnumerationDetect reconnaissance and unauthorized asset discovery.Unexpected enumeration of Modbus devices.
05 Remote Access & Lateral MovementDetect remote access or movement outside approved paths or scope.A vendor reaches an OT host outside the approved support window.
06 Network Path & Command ChannelsIdentify unapproved communication paths and command channels.A SCADA server connects to an unapproved external destination.
07 Collection, Staging & ExfiltrationDetect unauthorized gathering, staging, or transfer of industrial data.PLC project files are uploaded to an unapproved external destination.
08 Engineering & Configuration ChangeIdentify unauthorized changes to industrial engineering projects or configurations.An unauthorized PLC program download is observed.
09 Control, Data & Process IntegrityDetect unauthorized changes to commands, setpoints, or process data.A process setpoint changes outside approved limits.
10 View, Reporting & Alarm IntegrityDetect suppression or manipulation of operator displays, reports, and alarms.A critical HMI alarm is suppressed without approval.
11 Availability, Recovery & Safety ImpactIdentify disruption to operations, recovery, or safety functions.A controller enters STOP and the affected process loses control availability.

ATT&CK crosswalk: Category 05 often relates to Lateral Movement, 06 to Command and Control, and 07 to ICS Collection or Enterprise Exfiltration. Categories 08–11 can relate to Impair Process Control, Inhibit Response Function, or Impact. These are examples, not one-to-one mappings; assign technique IDs based on the evidence for each detection.

For example, an unauthorized controller program download belongs in category 08. If evidence also shows a changed process value, investigate category 09. Use category 11 for an observed disruption or loss of safety function; record potential consequences separately.

All categories01 Exposure & Initial Access02 Identity, Credentials & Privilege03 Execution & Runtime Activity04 Discovery & Enumeration05 Remote Access & Lateral Movement06 Network Path & Command Channels07 Collection, Staging & Exfiltration08 Engineering & Configuration Change09 Control, Data & Process Integrity10 View, Reporting & Alarm Integrity11 Availability, Recovery & Safety Impact

Exposure & Initial Access

T1190 · ATT&CK EnterpriseExploit Public-Facing Application2 detections · Show +· Hide −
Exploit request has a corroborated server-side effectLS-ENT-T1190-D01 coreSpecified

Exposure & Initial Access · LS-DF02 · Revision range-core-2026-10-29.1

What to detect

Bind a deliberately vulnerable externally reachable lab endpoint to its expected exploit behavior and independent server-side effect.

Detection logic

Bind a deliberately vulnerable externally reachable lab endpoint to its expected exploit behavior and independent server-side effect. A suspicious request or HTTP 500 alone is not successful exploitation. Missing required observations or policy yields an inconclusive result, not a proven attack.

Related techniques

Join an externally initiated web request to a child process/command on the server, using request or process attribution and approved application behavior..

Detection logic

Join an externally initiated web request to a child process/command on the server, using request or process attribution and approved application behavior. Missing required observations or policy yields an inconclusive result, not a proven attack.

Monitoring classificationExposure posture1 detections · Show +· Hide −
Internet exposure of an OT management or control serviceOT-DC-010 Proposed designWave 1

What to detect

Identify an OT service reachable through public addressing or published NAT without approved exposure.

Detection logic

Correlate existing exposure inventory, edge configuration and accepted inbound evidence. Classify as posture until actual unauthorized use is evidenced; do not initiate external scans.

Related techniques

Exposure posture · Conditional candidates, pending corroboration

Public reachability is exposure evidence. Identify actual adversary access through the internet-facing device before assigning the technique.

T0883 · Internet Accessible Device · ICS

Sources

Approved exposure assessment results, firewall/NAT configuration and OT inventory

Inventory crosswalk

  • LS-02 · Firewall (OT)
  • LS-18 · Network Traffic (SPAN/TAP/IDS) — Nozomi Networks, Dragos, Claroty, Microsoft Defender for IoT

Candidate evidence sources. Compare the input events and fields above with the telemetry collected at the site.

Input events

Collect the native events and observations named in the detection logic and source requirements.

Preserve action outcome and producer attribution where available. Use independent evidence for any claimed process or safety effect.

Normalization

public/private mapping, service, reachability evidence, exposure approval.

Logical fields from the design. Map them to the site schema and verify availability before rollout.

Context and policy

Approved remote-access gateways and monitoring probes; validate OEM service identification rather than labeling a port as HTTP.

Resolve asset and account roles, approved routes, scope and change windows. Set thresholds and correlation windows using site policy. Treat missing required telemetry or policy as inconclusive.

Validation

Verify an existing known exposure and approved gateway; distinguish configured NAT from demonstrated reachability.

Monitoring classificationWeb exploitation variants1 detections · Show +· Hide −
Web exploitation or web-shell sequence on an OT-adjacent serviceOT-DC-067 Proposed designWave 2

What to detect

Identify exploit attempts or a corroborated uploaded web file followed by server-side execution on an OT-supporting server.

Detection logic

Keep request-signature, error-spike and web-shell sequence variants distinct; correlate request, file creation, requested URL and web-server child process by host and timing.

Related techniques

Web exploitation variants · Conditional candidates, pending corroboration

Request signatures and error spikes are indicators. Corroborate exploitation against the public application or separately verify a functioning web shell.

Monitoring classificationVulnerability and configuration posture1 detections · Show +· Hide −
Unapproved security posture or vulnerability exposure changeOT-DC-073 Proposed designWave 2

What to detect

Track newly relevant vulnerabilities, unsupported configurations and overdue approved remediation on known OT assets.

Detection logic

Join passive/approved inventory and version evidence with advisory applicability and site remediation plan; distinguish missing version data from proven vulnerable applicability.

Related techniques

Vulnerability and configuration posture · Operational or investigation classification

Version and advisory applicability establish exposure or remediation status. Require observed exploitation behavior for any technique mapping.

Sources

Approved asset/software inventory, vendor advisories and change/risk register

Inventory crosswalk

No inventory source associated with this record.

Candidate evidence sources. Compare the input events and fields above with the telemetry collected at the site.

Preserve action outcome and producer attribution where available. Use independent evidence for any claimed process or safety effect.

Normalization

model/version, applicability, compensating controls, owner, due date.

Logical fields from the design. Map them to the site schema and verify availability before rollout.

Context and policy

Accepted risk, OEM constraints, compensating controls and unavailable maintenance windows.

Resolve asset and account roles, approved routes, scope and change windows. Set thresholds and correlation windows using site policy. Treat missing required telemetry or policy as inconclusive.

Validation

Test an applicable and nonapplicable advisory plus unknown version; use existing evidence rather than initiating an active scan.

Identity, Credentials & Privilege

T0859 · ATT&CK ICSValid Accounts11 detections · Show +· Hide −
First-time or rare OT asset accessLS-OT-T0859-D01 extendedDraft

Identity, Credentials & Privilege · LS-DF03 · Revision 2

What to detect

An account logs in to an OT device it rarely uses.

Detection logic

Compare the account and device with past logins. A new login in a short search period does not prove this is the first login ever.

Related techniques

T0859 · Valid Accounts · ICS

An attacker uses valid account credentials to access industrial systems. Their actions may look like normal user activity because the account has real permissions.

OT-DC-004
Access outside a normal work windowLS-OT-T0859-D02 coreDraft

Identity, Credentials & Privilege · LS-DF03 · Revision 2

What to detect

An account logs in outside its normal work or support hours.

Detection logic

Check the login time against the user’s shift and time zone. Check approved exceptions too. Fixed hours alone may miss valid work.

OT-DC-004
Unexpected source or access pathLS-OT-T0859-D03 coreDraft

Identity, Credentials & Privilege · LS-DF03 · Revision 2

What to detect

An account logs in from an unexpected device or skips an approved jump host.

Detection logic

Check whether this account may connect from this source to this target. Use the login source address, not the address of the device that sent the log. An allowed IP address only tells you about the source. To check whether the login skipped a jump host, also check the connection path or session logs.

Related techniques

T0859 · Valid Accounts · ICS

An attacker uses valid account credentials to access industrial systems. Their actions may look like normal user activity because the account has real permissions.

Sources

  • HMI
  • Jump Host
  • Engineering Workstation
  • VPN Gateway
  • Network

Input events

  • Successful login
  • Connection path or jump-host session records

  • user
  • auth_result
  • destination_host
  • destination_asset_role
  • source_ip
  • source_host
  • source_zone
  • approved_path
  • session_id

Context and policy

  • Approved source for each identity and target
  • Allowed network paths and jump hosts
  • Asset roles and approved replacements

OT-DC-004
One identity on multiple OT assetsLS-OT-T0859-D04 extendedDraft

Identity, Credentials & Privilege · LS-DF03 · Revision 2

What to detect

One account has active sessions on more OT devices than usual.

Detection logic

Count the devices used by the account. Check whether the sessions were active at the same time. Several logins close together do not prove this.

Service account used interactivelyLS-OT-T0859-D05 coreDraft

Identity, Credentials & Privilege · LS-DF03 · Revision 2

What to detect

A service account is used to log in and work by hand.

Detection logic

Check which accounts are service accounts. Look for login types that let a person work directly. An SSH connection alone does not prove this.

Detection logic

OT-DC-004
Failures, then success and OT accessLS-OT-T0859-D07 coreDraft

Identity, Credentials & Privilege · LS-DF03 · Revision 2

What to detect

An account fails to log in several times, then logs in and accesses an OT device.

Detection logic

Find failed logins followed by a successful login for the same user, source and target. Then check that the user accessed an OT device. A successful login alone is not enough.

Related techniques

An attacker uses valid account credentials to access industrial systems. Their actions may look like normal user activity because the account has real permissions.

OT-DC-004
Rapid lateral movement with valid accountsLS-OT-T0859-D09 extendedDraft

Identity, Credentials & Privilege · LS-DF03 · Revision 2

What to detect

An account moves between OT devices along an unusual route.

Detection logic

Follow the same account across devices. Compare the order of connections with allowed routes. A high device count alone is only a reason to investigate.

OT-DC-004
Login followed by an engineering changeLS-OT-T0859-D10 coreDraft

Identity, Credentials & Privilege · LS-DF03 · Revision 2

What to detect

An account logs in, then makes an important control or program change.

Detection logic

Link the login and change to the same account and session. Check session links when the action crosses devices. Check whether the change was approved before calling it an attack.

OT-DC-004
Dormant or shared OT account used outside authorized contextOT-DC-004 Proposed designWave 1

What to detect

Identify successful use of an OT account whose owner, source, role or reactivation approval does not match policy.

Detection logic

Join successful authentication with account lifecycle, owner, source history and ticket. Dormancy is configurable; escalate valid-account misuse only with corroboration such as owner denial or unauthorized action.

Related techniques

T0859 · Valid Accounts · ICS

Primary proposed behavior · Medium: valid-account misuse is conditional, not established by novelty

Official detection references

DET0724 · Detection of Valid Accounts

Sources

Authentication, IAM/PAM and account inventory

Inventory crosswalk

  • LS-20 · OT Identity / Directory
  • LS-04 · VPN Server
  • LS-03 · Jump Host

Candidate evidence sources. Compare the input events and fields above with the telemetry collected at the site.

Input events

  • Successful account activity with attributable session
  • Account role or lifecycle evidence
  • Approved scope and access window

Preserve action outcome and producer attribution where available. Use independent evidence for any claimed process or safety effect.

Normalization

account_id, enabled_state, last_success, owner, source_asset, target, ticket.

Logical fields from the design. Map them to the site schema and verify availability before rollout.

Context and policy

Commissioning accounts, infrequent maintenance, shift cover, shared-account attribution limits.

Resolve asset and account roles, approved routes, scope and change windows. Set thresholds and correlation windows using site policy. Treat missing required telemetry or policy as inconclusive.

Validation

Replay approved dormant-account reuse and unapproved reuse; verify unfamiliar source alone remains an anomaly.

Traceability

T1110 · ATT&CK EnterpriseBrute Force1 detections · Show +· Hide −
Authentication attack against OT access servicesOT-DC-003 Proposed designWave 1

What to detect

Detect password guessing against one account and password spraying across multiple accounts, with a separate escalation for a subsequent successful login.

Detection logic

Use separate variants: failures by target/account; distinct accounts by source; success after failures linked by account, target and session context. Configure count, distinct-account and time-window parameters per service.

Related techniques

Authentication abuse · Proposed Enterprise mapping

Repeated guessing or spraying is the behavior tested. A successful login is a separate outcome.

T1059.004 · ATT&CK EnterpriseUnix Shell2 detections · Show +· Hide −

Execution & Runtime Activity · LS-DF04 · Revision range-core-2026-10-29.1

What to detect

Require actual Unix shell process execution on the source host, parent/process/user context and a policy violation.

Detection logic

Require actual Unix shell process execution on the source host, parent/process/user context and a policy violation. A shell on the player workstation is not execution on a target asset. Missing required observations or policy yields an inconclusive result, not a proven attack.

Monitoring classificationProcess execution variants1 detections · Show +· Hide −

What to detect

Identify script, shell, living-off-the-land binary or executable behavior outside the host-role and administrator baseline.

Detection logic

Correlate process tree, command content, signer/hash, script source, user role and approval; encoded syntax or tool name alone is insufficient.

Related techniques

Process execution variants · Conditional candidates, pending corroboration

Process events may represent many interpreters or executables. A Unix shell variant requires confirmed Unix shell execution and workflow evidence.

What to detect

Identify an unapproved modification of an engineering project or replacement of a project with unexpected provenance.

Detection logic

Compare trusted repository version/signature/content against staged/opened project and permitted actor. A filename extension or transfer is insufficient to establish infection.

Related techniques

Engineering project provenance · Conditional candidates, pending corroboration

A changed project or unexpected signer establishes integrity deviation. Confirm a maliciously modified project capable of propagating through the engineering workflow.

T0873 · Project File Infection · ICS

Sources

Engineering project audit, file integrity and version repository

Inventory crosswalk

  • LS-08 · Workstations (Engineering/Operator)

Candidate evidence sources. Compare the input events and fields above with the telemetry collected at the site.

Input events

Collect the native events and observations named in the detection logic and source requirements.

Preserve action outcome and producer attribution where available. Use independent evidence for any claimed process or safety effect.

Normalization

project, hash/signature, changed blocks, source, actor, open/deploy event.

Logical fields from the design. Map them to the site schema and verify availability before rollout.

Context and policy

Approved project conversion, vendor tool upgrades, backups and metadata changes.

Resolve asset and account roles, approved routes, scope and change windows. Set thresholds and correlation windows using site policy. Treat missing required telemetry or policy as inconclusive.

Validation

Test unchanged transfers and approved edits against a known unauthorized project modification. Add T0873 only if project infection is independently demonstrated.

Control, Data & Process Integrity

T0831 · ATT&CK ICSManipulation of Control2 detections · Show +· Hide −
Unapproved control action changes the processLS-OT-T0831-D01 coreSpecified

Control, Data & Process Integrity · LS-DF12 · Revision range-core-2026-10-29.1

What to detect

Join a policy-violating control action with an independent process-state witness using the same target/object and a bounded causal window.

Detection logic

Join a policy-violating control action with an independent process-state witness using the same target/object and a bounded causal window. A command response alone is not process truth. Missing required observations or policy yields an inconclusive result, not a proven attack.

Related techniques

T0831 · Manipulation of Control · ICS

An attacker changes how a physical process runs, for example by changing setpoints or sending control commands.

Sources

  • PLC
  • RTU
  • HMI
  • SCADA

Input events

  • Physical process state changed
  • Native action outcome and attributable producer
  • Independent witness for any claimed process/service/view effect

Normalization · Required fields · 9

  • timestamp
  • actor.user
  • actor.ip
  • target.asset
  • outcome
  • object.id
  • value.before
  • value.after
  • witness.id

Context and policy

  • Approved actor/source/target policy for the exercise
  • Account and asset roles
  • Approved maintenance or diagnostic windows
  • Explicit timing, threshold and correlation-key policy

What to detect

A new or unapproved device acts as a master for a controller.

Detection logic

Find requests that show the source acting as a master. Check the approved sources for that controller and protocol. Missing approval data means unknown, not unapproved.

T1692.001 · ATT&CK ICSCommand Message16 detections · Show +· Hide −
Write from an unexpected sourceLS-OT-T1692.001-D01 coreDraft

Control, Data & Process Integrity · LS-DF12 · Revision pilot-2026-10-10.simple-1

What to detect

A new, unknown or disallowed source IP sends a write or control command.

Detection logic

Find a write or control command. Alert if the source IP is not allowed, is new for this target and protocol, or is not in the known source list. Do not wait for a reply, approval checks or a process change. Review the alert afterward.

Related techniques

Write to an important objectLS-OT-T1692.001-D03 coreDraft

Control, Data & Process Integrity · LS-DF12 · Revision pilot-2026-10-10.simple-1

What to detect

A write targets a register, tag or other object marked as important.

Detection logic

Find writes to objects in the important object list. Alert on every matching write, even from a known and allowed source. Do not check user permissions or wait for a value change. Review the reason for the write afterward.

What to detect

Detect a decoded Modbus write from a source not authorized for the target unit, address range or function.

Detection logic

Evaluate FC05/06/0F/10 and supported additional write operations against source-target-function-address authorization; join response and mapped point meaning.

Modbus communication restart or listen-only requestOT-DC-020 Proposed designWave 1

What to detect

Identify supported diagnostic operations capable of resetting or silencing controller communications.

Detection logic

Separate FC08 subfunction 0x0001 Restart Communications Option from 0x0004 Force Listen Only Mode; check approved requester and device/transport support. Verify communications effect separately.

Monitoring classificationSafety override policy1 detections · Show +· Hide −
Safety bypass, inhibit or override outside authorized scopeOT-DC-043 Proposed designWave 1

What to detect

Detect safety bypasses that lack approval, exceed their approved duration, or affect a prohibited combination of protection functions.

Detection logic

Track bypass lifecycle by SIF/tag, requester and approval; separate creation, expiry and simultaneous-bypass variants using engineering-defined limits.

Related techniques

Safety override policy · Conditional candidates, pending corroboration

An override outside approval establishes a safety policy violation. Confirm protection was actually lost and attributable to adversary action before mapping impact.

Sources

Normalization

SIF/tag, bypass state, activation/expiry, authorizer, operator, process mode.

Logical fields from the design. Map them to the site schema and verify availability before rollout.

Context and policy

Resolve asset and account roles, approved routes, scope and change windows. Set thresholds and correlation windows using site policy. Treat missing required telemetry or policy as inconclusive.

Monitoring classificationSafety margin1 detections · Show +· Hide −

What to detect

Provide an operational early-warning signal when a verified safety-related process variable approaches its engineered trip point.

Detection logic

Evaluate approved margin, trend and dwell for the current mode/SIF; correlate process commands, bypasses and controller changes without assuming causality.

Related techniques

Safety margin · Operational or investigation classification

A verified value approaching its trip point is an early warning. Validate actual loss of safety separately.

Monitoring classificationProtection demand and response1 detections · Show +· Hide −
Unexpected trip or missing expected protective responseOT-DC-048 Proposed designWave 2

What to detect

Identify an unexpected SIF demand/trip or a protective response absent when the validated trip conditions are met.

Detection logic

Maintain separate unexpected-trip and missing-response variants; evaluate voting, latching, permissives, delays and expected final-element response against approved design.

Related techniques

Protection demand and response · Conditional candidates, pending corroboration

Unexpected demand and missing response are distinct signals. Confirm actual loss of protection and causal evidence before using the impact technique.

Compare independent, time-aligned channels using engineered tolerances, quality and voting configuration; distinguish common-source data from true redundancy.

Monitoring classificationDevice and telemetry availability1 detections · Show +· Hide −
Loss of controller, SIS or remote-site telemetryOT-DC-051 Proposed designWave 1

What to detect

Identify missing reporting, controller restart, power event or gateway communication loss using expected asset-specific cadence.

Detection logic

Correlate collector health, link status, device diagnostics and expected production state; separate source outage, network loss and collection failure.

Related techniques

Device and telemetry availability · Conditional candidates, pending corroboration

Missing telemetry may be a device, link or collector fault. Confirm deliberate obstruction of the communication medium to map Block Communications.

Monitoring classificationDestructive activity and availability1 detections · Show +· Hide −
OT host file encryption or destructive file operationsOT-DC-058 Proposed designWave 1

What to detect

Identify destructive changes or encryption-like activity that threatens engineering and operational data availability.

Detection logic

Separate high-rate encryption/rename, file deletion, raw-disk writes and recovery-disable commands; correlate actual changes, process and affected assets. A precursor command is not proof of destruction.

Related techniques

Destructive activity and availability · Conditional candidates, pending corroboration

Encryption-like writes, deletion, raw disk changes and recovery commands are distinct. Confirm data destruction and its attributable producer before mapping.

Monitoring classificationLoad and service health1 detections · Show +· Hide −
Network volume, connection rate or service-exhaustion anomalyOT-DC-065 Proposed designWave 2

What to detect

Identify traffic or service load capable of degrading OT-adjacent availability, while separating load anomaly from demonstrated DoS.

Detection logic

Use distinct variants for bytes/connection rate, SYN flood, direct UDP, reflection/amplification, ICMP and multi-source fan-in; correlate resource/service health.

Related techniques

Load and service health · Conditional candidates, pending corroboration

High traffic is a load signal. Establish actual denial or an attributable denial attempt with the flood mechanism and service evidence.
